Section II. DESCRIPTION AND DATA

3. Purpose and Use                                 approximately +50 meters for 80 percent of
                                                   all locations made with the lower beam tilted
   Radar Set AN/MPQ-4A (fig. 1) is a mobile,       50 mils to the horizontal and with the shell
low-range radar system used to determine the       origin on the horizontal plane.
location of enemy mortars or low-velocity artil-
lery. The radar set is powered by Gasoline En-        b. Detecting. The radar set detects or inter-
gine Generator Set PU-107A/U, which is             cepts a projectile and provides accurate in-
mounted on a trailer. Provision is made for        formation as to its origin in range (distance)
local or remote operation. The radar set oper-     and azimuth (direction). The radar set also
ates in the 16,000-megacycle frequency (Ky)        supplies the easting and northing coordinates
band. The operating components of the AN/          of the weapon position. This data may be used
MPQ-4A are shown in figure 2. The functions        for the following purposes:
of the radar set are as follows:
                                                           (1) To determine the location of hidden
   a. Searching. The radar set is used as a                      enemy mortars or low-velocity artil-
sector search set to intercept the flight path of                lery pieces.
a low-velocity projectile (target) having a
minimum caliber of 60 millimeter (mm). Tar-               (2) To determine the impact point of
gets are detected and displayed on a B-scope.                   friendly (outgoing) mortar fire.
Accuracy of weapon location is designed to be
                                                          (3) To determine the impact point of
AGO 5427A                                                        enemy (incoming) mortar fire.

   m. Remove the waterproof cover from the
spirit level assembly (fig. 1). Level the radar
set by properly adjusting the elevation of the
jack screws until the level bubble is within
the inner scribed circle. Rotate the antenna
to other azimuth positions and check for level-
ness of the level bubble. The leveling operation
must be accomplished with a minimum exten-
sion of the outrigger jack screws to insure that
the available adjustment is not exhausted and
also to minimize antenna silhouette height.
The majority of the weight should be removed
from the trailer wheels to insure maximum
stability.

Section II. DEMOLITION OF MATERIEL TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

114. General                                      Use sledges, axes, pickaxes, hammers, crow-
                                                  bars, or other heavy tools.
   The demolition procedures outlined in para-
graph 115 will be used to prevent the enemy           b. Cut. Cut all cords, cables, and wiring.
from using or salvaging this equipment. Demo-     Use axes, handaxes, or machetes.
lition of the equipment will be accomplished
only upon order of the commander.                     c. Burn. Burn cords, resistors, capacitors,
                                                  coils, wiring, and technical manuals. Use gaso-
115. Methods of Destruction                       line, kerosene, oil, flame throwers, or incendiary
                                                  grenades.
   Use any or all methods described below to
destroy the equipment.                               d. Bend. Bend panels, cabinets, and chassis.
                                                     e. Hxplosives. Explosives are necessary to
   a. Smash. Smash the magnetron, klystron,       destroy the antenna group. Use firearms, gren-
waveguides, modulator transmitter, indicator      ades, or TNT.
CRT, computer, all tubes, meters, variable
capacitors, relays, spare parts, and power unit.     f. Disposal. Bury or scatter the destroyed
                                                  parts, in slit trenches, fox holes, or other holes,
                                                  or throw them into streams.
